Pretty much since that day at work when the pallet collapsed and my coworker got hurt. I keep thinking maybe it’ll just lift on its own, like if I get through another few days I’ll feel normal again, but it hasn’t really changed.\n\nIt’s not just one bad evening here and there. Most days, for most of the day, I feel like that flatness is there in the background. Sometimes I can distract myself a little, but when I stop and notice how I actually feel, it’s still there. It started after the accident, and it’s been hanging around since then.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, depressed, or hopeless during this time?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it depressed exactly. I mean… maybe a little down sometimes, but it doesn’t feel like sadness in the way I normally understand sadness. It’s more like I’m blank, if that makes sense.\n\nHopeless… I don’t know. I don’t think I’ve been walking around thinking there’s no point to anything or that things can’t get better. It’s more that I feel disconnected from the good feelings I’m used to having. So when you say “down or depressed,” I hesitate, because I don’t want to say yes if that means I’m crying all day or feeling despair, because that’s not really what it’s been like.\n\nIt’s more flat than sad. Like I know something is wrong with how I’m feeling, but I don’t know if I’d describe it as hopeless.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in your energy level or an increase in fatigue during this time?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I mean, I’m tired sometimes, but I don’t know if it’s a different kind of tired than just… life tired, if that makes sense.\n\nI still get up and go to work, and I still do what I need to do. I wouldn’t say I’m collapsing or sleeping all day or anything like that. But there are moments where I feel worn out in a way that’s hard to separate from just feeling kind of emotionally flat. Like I’ll sit down after work and think, “Am I exhausted, or do I just not want to do anything?” And I honestly can’t always tell.\n\nSo… I haven’t noticed some huge drop in energy, no. Not like I can clearly say, “Yes, my energy is much lower.” It’s more that everything feels heavier to start, but I don’t know if that’s fatigue exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight recently—like eating more or less than usual, or losing or gaining weight without trying?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in any obvious way. I mean, I haven’t been weighing myself every day or anything, so I can’t swear there’s been no change at all, but my clothes fit the same, and no one’s said anything to me about looking thinner or heavier.\n\nEating has been kind of normal, I guess. Some days I’m not that interested in food, but I still eat because it’s just part of the routine, and other days I’ll grab whatever’s easy after work. I wouldn’t say I’ve been eating a lot more or a lot less than usual. Nothing where I thought, “Oh wow, my appetite is really different.”\n\nSo… no, I haven’t really noticed a clear appetite or weight change.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any difficulty concentrating or thinking clearly lately, like it’s harder to focus or make decisions?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, not in a way that jumps out to me as, like, “Oh, I can’t think anymore” or anything. I still get through what I need to do, and I can follow conversations and do my job.\n\nSometimes I’ll feel a little foggy, but I’m not sure if that’s concentration exactly or just me being distracted or tired or kind of checked out. Like I might reread something or stand there for a second trying to remember what I was about to do, but that also feels like something people do normally, so I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is.\n\nDecisions… I guess I haven’t noticed a major change. I’m not sitting there unable to choose what to eat or what to do. Maybe I’m slower with things sometimes, but nothing that feels really clear or dramatic. So I’d say no, not really, or at least not that I’ve clearly noticed.\n\nDoctor: Has there been a time recently when you felt no pleasure or interest in almost any activity, even the ones you usually enjoy the most?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes. That’s actually probably the thing that scared me enough to come in, if I’m being honest.\n\nIt’s not exactly that I don’t do anything. I still sit with my husband, and I’ll still turn on one of my shows after work because that’s what I normally do, and I’m trying to keep things feeling normal. But it’s like… the warm part of it is gone. Like I know, in my head, that sitting next to him should feel comforting, or that a show I usually like should at least feel distracting or a little nice, but I’m just sitting there and there’s nothing really coming back at me.\n\nAnd that feels really strange, because it’s not even always sadness exactly. It’s more like I’m waiting for the feeling to show up and it doesn’t. I’ll think, “Okay, this is the part where I usually relax,” or “This is where I usually laugh,” and instead I just feel kind of blank. Like I’m doing the motions of things I normally enjoy, but I’m not actually getting the good feeling from them.\n\nSo… yes, I have noticed that. Not that I’ve stopped caring about everything forever or anything, but recently it’s been hard to feel pleasure or comfort from the things that usually would give me that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your thoughts are slowing down or that you're moving more slowly than usual lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve noticed that, no. I mean… I don’t think anyone would look at me and say I’m moving slowly or talking slowly or anything like that. I still get up, get dressed, go through my day, do the things I need to do.\n\nThere are moments where I feel kind of… heavy, I guess, or like it takes effort to get myself started, but I don’t know if that’s the same thing as actually moving slower. Once I’m doing something, I think I’m moving pretty normally. And with my thoughts, I don’t think they’re slowed down exactly. Sometimes I pause or lose my train of thought for a second, but it doesn’t feel like my brain is in slow motion.\n\nSo I’d say no, not clearly. I haven’t really noticed that my thoughts or movements are slower than usual in a way I could point to.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that nothing you do really feels pleasurable anymore, even things you used to enjoy a lot, like they don’t have any emotional impact on you at all?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, that’s pretty close to how it feels. I wouldn’t say literally nothing ever has any impact, because I don’t want to overstate it, but honestly, what made me come in is that it feels like the warm part of me kind of shut off.\n\nLike after work, I’ll sit with my husband, and normally that would feel grounding or comforting just being near him. Or I’ll put on one of my shows, the kind of thing I usually look forward to because it helps me unwind. And I know in my head, “This should feel nice,” or “This is usually when I relax,” but emotionally it’s like… nothing really lands. It’s just flat. I’m there, I’m doing the thing, but I’m not getting the comfort or the little bit of happiness from it.\n\nThat’s the part that bothers me, because I’m not choosing to be distant or uninterested. I want to feel it. I keep waiting for that normal feeling to come back, and it just doesn’t really show up.\n\nDoctor: Has there been a noticeable drop in your ability to feel pleasure or interest that’s lasted for most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, that’s how it’s felt. I’m not good at judging the exact time frame, like counting the days perfectly, but it’s been enough and often enough that it doesn’t feel like just one bad evening or a mood that passes after a night’s sleep.\n\nHonestly, what made me come in is that I feel like the warm part of me shut off. I’ll sit with my husband after work, and I know that should feel comforting. Like, I know him being next to me should make me feel safe or settled or just… normal. And I’ll put on my shows, the ones I usually like, the ones I usually use to unwind, and I keep expecting that little feeling of relief or enjoyment to happen.\n\nBut most of the time there’s just nothing there. I’m watching, I’m sitting there, I’m going through the motions, and in my head I know, “This is supposed to feel good,” but emotionally it’s flat. It’s not that I don’t want to enjoy it. I really do. I keep trying to sort of coax the feeling back, if that makes sense, but it doesn’t really come.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you can no longer feel any pleasure or interest in things you used to enjoy, even for a brief moment, most days?\n\nPatient: Most days, yes… I think that’s fair to say. I don’t want to say it’s absolutely every single second, like I’m incapable of noticing anything at all, but the pleasure part is just… mostly not there.\n\nLike I’ll sit with my husband after work, and I know normally that should feel comforting. Even just being next to him, talking a little, or sitting quietly together — that used to make me feel settled, like I could breathe a bit. And now I’m there, and I know in my head that I love him and that this should feel good, but emotionally it’s like nothing connects.\n\nSame with my shows. I’ll put them on because that’s what I usually do to unwind, and I keep waiting for that little bit of interest or comfort to kick in. But it’s flat. It’s like I’m watching myself do something I used to enjoy, instead of actually enjoying it.\n\nSo… yes, even brief moments of real pleasure or interest are hard to find most days. Honestly, that’s a big part of why I came in — it feels like the warm part of me shut off.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that even things that used to make you smile or feel even a little happy don’t have any effect on your mood at all anymore, like they just don’t register emotionally?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s exactly it. I keep trying to explain it without sounding dramatic, but it feels like the warm part of me just shut off.\n\nLike after work, I’ll sit with my husband, and normally even just being near him would make me feel a little calmer or comforted, maybe even smile at something small he says. And now I’m sitting there, and I know in my head, “This is my husband, I love him, this should feel good,” but it doesn’t really register in my body or my mood. It’s like the feeling doesn’t make it all the way through.\n\nAnd I’ll put on my shows, the ones I usually use to unwind, and I’ll be waiting for that little bit of relief or happiness, even just interest, and there’s just… nothing. I can recognize that something should be funny or nice, but it doesn’t lift me. It doesn’t change how I feel. That’s honestly one of the biggest reasons I came in, because it scares me how flat everything feels.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that even things that used to make you smile or feel even a little happy don’t have any effect on your mood at all anymore, like they just don’t register emotionally?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, yes. That’s probably one of the clearest ways to put it. It’s not like I don’t understand that something is supposed to be nice, or that I don’t know what I used to feel. I do know. That’s almost what makes it feel worse, because I can remember that those things used to reach me.\n\nLike after work, I’ll sit with my husband, and normally just being near him would make me feel a little comforted, even if the day was hard. I’d feel some kind of warmth, or safety, or just that little “okay, I’m home” feeling. And now I’m sitting there, and I know in my head that I love him, I know this is supposed to feel good, but emotionally it’s like it doesn’t land. It’s just blank.\n\nAnd I’ll put on my shows because that’s what I’ve always done to unwind, and I keep waiting for even a small reaction — like a laugh, or interest, or just feeling distracted in a good way — and it’s not there. I’m watching, but it doesn’t change my mood. It doesn’t lift anything.\n\nHonestly, that’s part of what scared me enough to come in. It feels like the warm part of me shut off.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… I think so. I mean, “completely” is a hard word, because part of me wants to leave room for maybe there are tiny moments I’m missing, but honestly, what made me come in is that it feels like the warm part of me shut off.

I’ll come home from work and sit with my husband, and I know I should feel comforted just being there with him. I know I love him. I know normally that would settle me or make me feel a little safe or even just… human, I guess. But I’m sitting there and it’s like nothing happens inside. It doesn’t reach me.

And I keep trying to watch my shows after work, because that’s always been my way to relax, and I’ll be looking at the screen waiting to feel interested or distracted or maybe laugh at something, and there’s just nothing there. It’s not that I don’t understand what should be enjoyable. I do. It just doesn’t affect my mood the way it used to.

So yes, the things that used to make me feel even a little happy or comforted don’t really seem to register right now. It’s very flat.
